Original: with  cross_items as  (select i_item_sk ss_item_sk  from item,  (select iss.i_brand_id brand_id      ,iss.i_class_id class_id      ,iss.i_category_id category_id  from store_sales      ,item Rewritten: WITH
store_base AS (
    SELECT ss.ss_quantity AS quantity,
           ss.ss_list_price AS list_price,
           d.d_week_seq,
           i.i_brand_id,
           i.i_class_id,
           i.i_category_id,
           i.i_category,
           i.i_manager_id
    FROM store_sales ss
    JOIN item i ON ss.ss_item_sk = i.i_item_sk
    JOIN date_dim d ON ss.ss_sold_date_sk = d.d_date_sk
    WHERE d.d_year BETWEEN 1998 AND 2000
      AND ss.ss_wholesale_cost BETWEEN 80 AND 100
),
catalog_base AS (
    SELECT cs.cs_quantity AS quantity,
           cs.cs_list_price AS list_price,
           i.i_brand_id,
           i.i_class_id,
           i.i_category_id,
           i.i_category,
           i.i_manager_id
    FROM catalog_sales cs
    JOIN item i ON cs.cs_item_sk = i.i_item_sk
    JOIN date_dim d ON cs.cs_sold_date_sk = d.d_date_sk
    WHERE d.d_year BETWEEN 1998 AND 2000
      AND cs.cs_wholesale_cost BETWEEN 80 AND 100
),
web_base AS (
    SELECT ws.ws_quantity AS quantity,
           ws.ws_list_price AS list_price,
           i.i_brand_id,
           i.i_class_id,
           i.i_category_id
    FROM web_sales ws
    JOIN item i ON ws.ws_item_sk = i.i_item_sk
    JOIN date_dim d ON ws.ws_sold_date_sk = d.d_date_sk
    WHERE d.d_year BETWEEN 1998 AND 2000
      AND ws.ws_wholesale_cost BETWEEN 80 AND 100
),
cross_keys AS (
    SELECT i_brand_id AS brand_id,
           i_class_id AS class_id,
           i_category_id AS category_id
    FROM store_base
    WHERE i_category IN ('Books', 'Home', 'Jewelry')
      AND i_manager_id BETWEEN 28 AND 37
    INTERSECT
    SELECT i_brand_id,
           i_class_id,
           i_category_id
    FROM catalog_base
    WHERE i_category IN ('Books', 'Home', 'Jewelry')
      AND i_manager_id BETWEEN 28 AND 37
    INTERSECT
    SELECT i_brand_id,
           i_class_id,
           i_category_id
    FROM web_base
),
avg_sales AS (
    SELECT AVG(quantity * list_price) AS average_sales
    FROM (
        SELECT quantity, list_price FROM store_base
        UNION ALL
        SELECT quantity, list_price FROM catalog_base
        UNION ALL
        SELECT quantity, list_price FROM web_base
    ) x
),
date_weeks AS (
    SELECT MAX(CASE WHEN d_year = 1999 AND d_moy = 12 AND d_dom = 5 THEN d_week_seq END) AS this_week_seq,
           MAX(CASE WHEN d_year = 1998 AND d_moy = 12 AND d_dom = 5 THEN d_week_seq END) AS last_week_seq
    FROM date_dim
),
agg AS (
    SELECT sb.i_brand_id,
           sb.i_class_id,
           sb.i_category_id,
           SUM(CASE WHEN sb.d_week_seq = w.this_week_seq THEN sb.quantity * sb.list_price END) AS ty_sales,
           COUNT(CASE WHEN sb.d_week_seq = w.this_week_seq THEN 1 END) AS ty_number_sales,
           SUM(CASE WHEN sb.d_week_seq = w.last_week_seq THEN sb.quantity * sb.list_price END) AS ly_sales,
           COUNT(CASE WHEN sb.d_week_seq = w.last_week_seq THEN 1 END) AS ly_number_sales
    FROM store_base sb
    JOIN cross_keys ck
      ON sb.i_brand_id = ck.brand_id
     AND sb.i_class_id = ck.class_id
     AND sb.i_category_id = ck.category_id
    CROSS JOIN date_weeks w
    WHERE sb.i_category IN ('Books', 'Home', 'Jewelry')
      AND sb.i_manager_id BETWEEN 28 AND 37
      AND sb.d_week_seq IN (w.this_week_seq, w.last_week_seq)
    GROUP BY sb.i_brand_id, sb.i_class_id, sb.i_category_id
)
SELECT 'store' AS ty_channel,
       a.i_brand_id AS ty_brand,
       a.i_class_id AS ty_class,
       a.i_category_id AS ty_category,
       a.ty_sales,
       a.ty_number_sales,
       'store' AS ly_channel,
       a.i_brand_id AS ly_brand,
       a.i_class_id AS ly_class,
       a.i_category_id AS ly_category,
       a.ly_sales,
       a.ly_number_sales
FROM agg a
CROSS JOIN avg_sales av
WHERE a.ty_sales > av.average_sales
  AND a.ly_sales > av.average_sales
ORDER BY ty_channel, ty_brand, ty_class, ty_category
LIMIT 100;

Let's write. cross_items is a CTE with potentially many rows, joining might be okay. But if cross_items is small, joining is good. If it's large, hash join. The original used IN with HashAggregate. This might use a semi-join and avoid duplicates even if cross_items had duplicates. But we know it's unique. Using JOIN might be more efficient because it can filter early. I'll use JOIN.

Now, we need present optimization strategies with scores. Let's list:
1. Combine this_year/last_year aggregations into a single scan using conditional aggregation and eliminate self-join. Score: 8/10. Reduces store_sales scan and self-join.
2. Replace three-way INTERSECT in cross_items with explicit DISTINCT CTEs and INNER JOINs, and push category filter to web_brands. Score: 7/10. Allows hash joins, reduces sort overhead, reduces web rows.
3. Replace `IN (SELECT ss_item_sk FROM cross_items)` with JOIN to cross_items (since unique). Score: 6/10. Avoids HashAggregate dedup.
4. Precompute target weeks in a CTE and join, avoiding repeated scalar subqueries. Score: 5/10. Minor.
5. Use CTEs for avg_sales (already there). Keep as is. Score: 3/10.
6. Add indexes (not SQL rewrite) on sales tables (wholesale_cost, sold_date_sk) and item (category, manager, brand, class) etc. Score: 9/10 but not SQL rewrite. Since mission is query rewriting, we can mention as recommendation.

```sql
WITH store_brands AS (
    SELECT DISTINCT iss.i_brand_id, iss.i_class_id, iss.i_category_id
    FROM store_sales ss
    JOIN item iss ON ss.ss_item_sk = iss.i_item_sk
    JOIN date_dim d1 ON ss.ss_sold_date_sk = d1.d_date_sk
    WHERE d1.d_year BETWEEN 1998 AND 2000
      AND iss.i_category IN ('Books', 'Home', 'Jewelry')
      AND iss.i_manager_id BETWEEN 28 AND 37
      AND ss.ss_wholesale_cost BETWEEN 80 AND 100
),
catalog_brands AS (
    SELECT DISTINCT ics.i_brand_id, ics.i_class_id, ics.i_category_id
    FROM catalog_sales cs
    JOIN item ics ON cs.cs_item_sk = ics.i_item_sk
    JOIN date_dim d2 ON cs.cs_sold_date_sk = d2.d_date_sk
    WHERE d2.d_year BETWEEN 1998 AND 2000
      AND ics.i_category IN ('Books', 'Home', 'Jewelry')
      AND ics.i_manager_id BETWEEN 28 AND 37
      AND cs.cs_wholesale_cost BETWEEN 80 AND 100
),
web_brands AS (
    SELECT DISTINCT iws.i_brand_id, iws.i_class_id, iws.i_category_id
    FROM web_sales ws
    JOIN item iws ON ws.ws_item_sk = iws.i_item_sk
    JOIN date_dim d3 ON ws.ws_sold_date_sk = d3.d_date_sk
    WHERE d3.d_year BETWEEN 1998 AND 2000
      AND iws.i_category IN ('Books', 'Home', 'Jewelry')  -- safe due to intersection with store/catalog
      AND ws.ws_wholesale_cost BETWEEN 80 AND 100
),
cross_triples AS (
    SELECT s.i_brand_id, s.i_class_id, s.i_category_id
    FROM store_brands s
    JOIN catalog_brands c
      ON s.i_brand_id = c.i_brand_id
     AND s.i_class_id = c.i_class_id
     AND s.i_category_id = c.i_category_id
    JOIN web_brands w
      ON s.i_brand_id = w.i_brand_id
     AND s.i_class_id = w.i_class_id
     AND s.i_category_id = w.i_category_id
),
cross_items AS (
    SELECT i.i_item_sk
    FROM item i
    JOIN cross_triples ct
      ON i.i_brand_id = ct.i_brand_id
     AND i.i_class_id = ct.i_class_id
     AND i.i_category_id = ct.i_category_id
    WHERE i.i_category IN ('Books', 'Home', 'Jewelry')
      AND i.i_manager_id BETWEEN 28 AND 37
),
avg_sales AS (
    SELECT AVG(quantity * list_price) AS average_sales
    FROM (
        SELECT ss_quantity AS quantity, ss_list_price AS list_price
        FROM store_sales
        JOIN date_dim ON ss_sold_date_sk = d_date_sk
        WHERE d_year BETWEEN 1998 AND 2000
          AND ss_wholesale_cost BETWEEN 80 AND 100
        UNION ALL
        SELECT cs_quantity, cs_list_price
        FROM catalog_sales
        JOIN date_dim ON cs_sold_date_sk = d_date_sk
        WHERE d_year BETWEEN 1998 AND 2000
          AND cs_wholesale_cost BETWEEN 80 AND 100
        UNION ALL
        SELECT ws_quantity, ws_list_price
        FROM web_sales
        JOIN date_dim ON ws_sold_date_sk = d_date_sk
        WHERE d_year BETWEEN 1998 AND 2000
          AND ws_wholesale_cost BETWEEN 80 AND 100
    ) x
),
target_weeks AS (
    SELECT d_week_seq,
           CASE WHEN d_year = 1999 THEN 'this' ELSE 'last' END AS year_type
    FROM date_dim
    WHERE (d_year = 1999 AND d_moy = 12 AND d_dom = 5)
       OR (d_year = 1998 AND d_moy = 12 AND d_dom = 5)
),
combined_sales AS (
    SELECT i.i_brand_id,
           i.i_class_id,
           i.i_category_id,
           SUM(CASE WHEN tw.year_type = 'this' THEN ss

=== Worker 0: Start Rewrite ===
.ss_quantity * ss.ss_list_price ELSE 0 END) AS ty_sales,
           SUM(CASE WHEN tw.year_type = 'this' THEN 1 ELSE 0 END) AS ty_number_sales,
           SUM(CASE WHEN tw.year_type = 'last' THEN ss.ss_quantity * ss.ss_list_price ELSE 0 END) AS ly_sales,
           SUM(CASE WHEN tw.year_type = 'last' THEN 1 ELSE 0 END) AS ly_number_sales
    FROM store_sales ss
    JOIN item i ON ss.ss_item_sk = i.i_item_sk
    JOIN date_dim d ON ss.ss_sold_date_sk = d.d_date_sk
    JOIN target_weeks tw ON d.d_week_seq = tw.d_week_seq
    JOIN cross_items ci ON ss.ss_item_sk = ci.ss_item_sk
    WHERE i.i_category IN ('Books', 'Home', 'Jewelry')
      AND i.i_manager_id BETWEEN 28 AND 37
      AND ss.ss_wholesale_cost BETWEEN 80 AND 100
    GROUP BY i.i_brand_id, i.i_class_id, i.i_category_id
)
SELECT 'store' AS ty_channel,
       cs.i_brand_id AS ty_brand,
       cs.i_class_id AS ty_class,
       cs.i_category_id AS ty_category,
       cs.ty_sales AS ty_sales,
       cs.ty_number_sales AS ty_number_sales,
       'store' AS ly_channel,
       cs.i_brand_id AS ly_brand,
       cs.i_class_id AS ly_class,
       cs.i_category_id AS ly_category,
       cs.ly_sales AS ly_sales,
       cs.ly_number_sales AS ly_number_sales
FROM combined_sales cs
CROSS JOIN avg_sales av
WHERE cs.ty_sales > av.average_sales
  AND cs.ly_sales > av.average_sales
ORDER BY ty_channel, ty_brand, ty_class, ty_category
LIMIT 100;
```

WITH avg_sales AS (
    SELECT AVG(quantity * list_price) AS average_sales
    FROM (
        SELECT ss.ss_quantity AS quantity, ss.ss_list_price AS list_price
        FROM store_sales ss
        JOIN date_dim d ON ss.ss_sold_date_sk = d.d_date_sk
        WHERE d.d_year BETWEEN 1998 AND 2000
          AND ss.ss_wholesale_cost BETWEEN 80 AND 100
        UNION ALL
        SELECT cs.cs_quantity, cs.cs_list_price
        FROM catalog_sales cs
        JOIN date_dim d ON cs.cs_sold_date_sk = d.d_date_sk

          AND cs.cs_wholesale_cost BETWEEN 80 AND 100
        UNION ALL
        SELECT ws.ws_quantity, ws.ws_list_price
        FROM web_sales ws
        JOIN date_dim d ON ws.ws_sold_date_sk = d.d_date_sk

cross_items AS (
    SELECT i.i_item_sk
    FROM item i
    JOIN cross_triples ct
      ON i.i_brand_id = ct.i_brand_id
     AND i.i_class_id = ct.i_class_id
     AND i.i_category_id = ct.i_category_id
    WHERE i.i_category IN ('Books','Home','Jewelry')
      AND i.i_manager_id BETWEEN 28 AND 37
),
avg_sales AS (
    SELECT AVG(quantity * list_price) AS average_sales
    FROM (
        SELECT ss_quantity AS quantity, ss_list_price AS list_price
        FROM store_sales
        JOIN date_dim ON ss_sold_date_sk = d_date_sk
        WHERE d_year BETWEEN 1998 AND 2000
          AND ss_wholesale_cost BETWEEN 80 AND 100
        UNION ALL
        SELECT cs_quantity, cs_list_price
        FROM catalog_sales
        JOIN date_dim ON cs_sold_date_sk = d_date_sk
        WHERE d_year BETWEEN 1998 AND 2000
          AND cs_wholesale_cost BETWEEN 80 AND 100
        UNION ALL
        SELECT ws_quantity, ws_list_price
        FROM web_sales
        JOIN date_dim ON ws_sold_date_sk = d_date_sk
        WHERE d_year BETWEEN 1998 AND 2000
          AND ws_wholesale_cost BETWEEN 80 AND 100
    ) x
),
target_weeks AS (
    SELECT d_week_seq,
           CASE WHEN d_year = 1999 THEN 'this' ELSE 'last' END AS year_type
    FROM date_dim
    WHERE (d_year = 1999 AND d_moy = 12 AND d_dom = 5)
       OR (d_year = 1998 AND d_moy = 12 AND d_dom = 5)
),
combined_sales AS (
    SELECT i.i_brand_id,
           i.i_class_id,
           i.i_category_id,
           SUM(CASE WHEN tw.year_type = 'this' THEN ss.ss_quantity * ss.ss_list_price ELSE 0 END) AS ty_sales,
           SUM(CASE WHEN tw.year_type = 'this' THEN 1 ELSE 0 END) AS ty_number_sales,
           SUM(CASE WHEN tw.year_type = 'last' THEN ss.ss_quantity * ss.ss_list_price ELSE 0 END) AS ly_sales,
           SUM(CASE WHEN tw.year_type = 'last' THEN 1 ELSE 0 END) AS ly_number_sales
    FROM store_sales ss
    JOIN item i ON ss.ss_item_sk = i.i_item_sk
    JOIN date_dim d ON ss.ss_sold_date_sk = d.d_date_sk
    JOIN target_weeks tw ON d.d_week_seq = tw.d_week_seq
    JOIN cross_items ci ON ss.ss_item_sk = ci.ss_item_sk
    WHERE i.i_category IN ('Books','Home','Jewelry')
      AND i.i_manager_id BETWEEN 28 AND 37
      AND ss.ss_wholesale_cost BETWEEN 80 AND 100
    GROUP BY i.i_brand_id, i.i_class_id, i.i_category_id
)
SELECT 'store' AS ty_channel,
       cs.i_brand_id AS ty_brand,
       cs.i_class_id AS ty_class,
       cs.i_category_id AS ty_category,
       cs.ty_sales AS ty_sales,
       cs.ty_number_sales AS ty_number_sales,
       'store' AS ly_channel,
       cs.i_brand_id AS ly_brand,
       cs.i_class_id AS ly_class,
       cs.i_category_id AS ly_category,
       cs.ly_sales AS ly_sales,
       cs.ly_number_sales AS ly_number_sales
FROM combined_sales cs
CROSS JOIN avg_sales av
WHERE cs.ty_sales > av.average_sales
  AND cs.ly_sales > av.average_sales
  AND cs.ty_number_sales > 0
  AND cs.ly_number_sales > 0
ORDER BY ty_channel, ty_brand, ty_class, ty_category
LIMIT 100;

        "produced_suggestion": "Combined the this_year and last_year aggregations into a single scan of store_sales using conditional aggregation (CASE) on a precomputed target_weeks CTE, removing the duplicate store_sales scan and the self-join of two nearly identical subqueries."
    },
    {
        "group": "Join_optimization",
        "produced_suggestion": "Replaced the `IN (SELECT ss_item_sk FROM cross_items)` with a direct JOIN to cross_items; since cross_items yields unique i_item_sk values, this avoids the HashAggregate deduplication step and allows better join ordering."
    },
    {
        "group": "Predicate_simplification",
        "produced_suggestion": "Precomputed both target week sequences (Dec 5 of 1998 and 1999) in a small target_weeks CTE, replacing repeated scalar subqueries in the outer WHERE clauses; constant folding of `1998 + 2` to 2000 throughout."
    },
    {
        "group": "Constant_folding",
        "produced_suggestion": "Folded date arithmetic `1998 + 1` = 1999 and `1998 + 2` = 2000 in all date filters and the target week subqueries."
    }

</advice>

</analysis>
The original TPC-DS query (Q39-like) scans store_sales four times (once in cross_items, once in avg_sales, twice for this_year/last_year) and computes a costly three-way INTERSECT. The rewritten query:
1. Replaces the INTERSECT with DISTINCT per-channel CTEs joined together — same set semantics, better hash-join planning.
2. Merges the two nearly identical year subqueries into one pass over store_sales via conditional aggregation against a tiny target_weeks CTE, eliminating one store_sales scan and the self-join.
3. Uses a direct join to cross_items (unique i_item_sk guaranteed because item.i_item_sk is unique and cross_triples is distinct) instead of the IN-subquery dedup.
4. Adds ty_number_sales/ly_number_sales > 0 guards to preserve the "group exists in both years" semantics of the original inner-join regardless of avg_sales value; folds all date constants. Equivalence to the original result set is preserved including HAVING > avg_sales per year and the Dec-5-week definitions.
</analysis>

</enhanced_sql>
```sql
WITH store_brands AS (
    SELECT DISTINCT iss.i_brand_id, iss.i_class_id, iss.i_category_id
    FROM store_sales ss
    JOIN item iss ON ss.ss_item_sk = iss.i_item_sk
    JOIN date_dim d1 ON ss.ss_sold_date_sk = d1.d_date_sk
    WHERE d1.d_year BETWEEN 1998 AND 2000
      AND iss.i_category IN ('Books','Home','Jewelry')
      AND iss.i_manager_id BETWEEN 28 AND 37
      AND ss.ss_wholesale_cost BETWEEN 80 AND 100
),
catalog_brands AS (
    SELECT DISTINCT ics.i_brand_id, ics.i_class_id, ics.i_category_id
    FROM catalog_sales cs
    JOIN item ics ON cs.cs_item_sk = ics.i_item_sk
    JOIN date_dim d2 ON cs.cs_sold_date_sk = d2.d_date_sk
    WHERE d2.d_year BETWEEN 1998 AND 2000
      AND ics.i_category IN ('Books','Home','Jewelry')
      AND ics.i_manager_id BETWEEN 28 AND 37
      AND cs.cs_wholesale_cost BETWEEN 80 AND 100
),
web_brands AS (
    SELECT DISTINCT iws.i_brand_id, iws.i_class_id, iws.i_category_id
    FROM web_sales ws
    JOIN item iws ON ws.ws_item_sk = iws.i_item_sk
    JOIN date_dim d3 ON ws.ws_sold_date_sk = d3.d_date_sk
    WHERE d3.d_year BETWEEN 1998 AND 2000
      AND ws.ws_wholesale_cost BETWEEN 80 AND 100
),
cross_triples AS (
    SELECT s.i_brand_id, s.i_class_id, s.i_category_id
    FROM store_brands s
    JOIN catalog_brands c
      ON s.i_brand_id = c.i_brand_id
     AND s.i_class_id = c.i_class_id
     AND s.i_category_id = c.i_category_id
    JOIN web_brands w
      ON s.i_brand_id = w.i_brand_id
     AND s.i_class_id = w.i_class_id
     AND s.i_category_id = w.i_category_id
),
cross_items AS (
    SELECT i.i_item_sk
    FROM item i
    JOIN cross_triples ct
      ON i.i_brand_id = ct.i_brand_id
     AND i.i_class_id = ct.i_class_id
     AND i.i_category_id = ct.i_category_id
    WHERE i.i_category IN ('Books','Home','Jewelry')
      AND i.i_manager_id BETWEEN 28 AND 37
),
avg_sales AS (
    SELECT AVG(quantity * list_price) AS average_sales
    FROM (
        SELECT ss_quantity AS quantity, ss_list_price AS list_price
        FROM store_sales
        JOIN date_dim ON ss_sold_date_sk = d_date_sk
        WHERE d_year BETWEEN 1998 AND 2000
          AND ss_wholesale_cost BETWEEN 80 AND 100
        UNION ALL
        SELECT cs_quantity, cs_list_price
        FROM catalog_sales
        JOIN date_dim ON cs_sold_date_sk = d_date_sk
        WHERE d_year BETWEEN 1998 AND 2000
          AND cs_wholesale_cost BETWEEN 80 AND 100
        UNION ALL
        SELECT ws_quantity, ws_list_price
        FROM web_sales
        JOIN date_dim ON ws_sold_date_sk = d_date_sk
        WHERE d_year BETWEEN 1998 AND 2000
          AND ws_wholesale_cost BETWEEN 80 AND 100
    ) x
),
target_weeks AS (
    SELECT d_week_seq,
           CASE WHEN d_year = 1999 THEN 'this' ELSE 'last' END AS year_type
    FROM date_dim
    WHERE (d_year = 1999 AND d_moy = 12 AND d_dom = 5)
       OR (d_year = 1998 AND d_moy = 12 AND d_dom = 5)
),
combined_sales AS (
    SELECT i.i_brand_id,
           i.i_class_id,
           i.i_category_id,
           SUM(CASE WHEN tw.year_type = 'this' THEN ss.ss_quantity * ss.ss_list_price ELSE 0 END) AS ty_sales,
           SUM(CASE WHEN tw.year_type = 'this' THEN 1 ELSE 0 END) AS ty_number_sales,
           SUM(CASE WHEN tw.year_type = 'last' THEN ss.ss_quantity * ss.ss_list_price ELSE 0 END) AS ly_sales,
           SUM(CASE WHEN tw.year_type = 'last' THEN 1 ELSE 0 END) AS ly_number_sales
    FROM store_sales ss
    JOIN item i ON ss.ss_item_sk = i.i_item_sk
    JOIN date_dim d ON ss.ss_sold_date_sk = d.d_date_sk
    JOIN target_weeks tw ON d.d_week_seq = tw.d_week_seq
    JOIN cross_items ci ON ss.ss_item_sk = ci.ss_item_sk
    WHERE i.i_category IN ('Books','Home','Jewelry')
      AND i.i_manager_id BETWEEN 28 AND 37
      AND ss.ss_wholesale_cost BETWEEN 80 AND 100
    GROUP BY i.i_brand_id, i.i_class_id, i.i_category_id
)
SELECT 'store' AS ty_channel,
       cs.i_brand_id AS ty_brand,
       cs.i_class_id AS ty_class,
       cs.i_category_id AS ty_category,
       cs.ty_sales AS ty_sales,
       cs.ty_number_sales AS ty_number_sales,
       'store' AS ly_channel,
       cs.i_brand_id AS ly_brand,
       cs.i_class_id AS ly_class,
       cs.i_category_id AS ly_category,
       cs.ly_sales AS ly_sales,
       cs.ly_number_sales AS ly_number_sales
FROM combined_sales cs
CROSS JOIN avg_sales av
WHERE cs.ty_sales > av.average_sales
  AND cs.ly_sales > av.average_sales
  AND cs.ty_number_sales > 0
  AND cs.ly_number_sales > 0
ORDER BY ty_channel, ty_brand, ty_class, ty_category
LIMIT 100;
```

WITH store_brands AS (

WITH store_brands AS (
    SELECT DISTINCT iss.i_brand_id, iss.i_class_id, iss.i_category_id
    FROM store_sales ss
    JOIN item iss ON ss.ss_item_sk = iss.i_item_sk
    JOIN date_dim d1 ON ss.ss_sold_date_sk = d1.d_date_sk
    WHERE d1.d_year BETWEEN 1998 AND 2000
      AND iss.i_category IN ('Books','Home','Jewelry')
      AND iss.i_manager_id BETWEEN 28 AND 37
      AND ss.ss_wholesale_cost BETWEEN 80 AND 100
),
catalog_brands AS (
    SELECT DISTINCT ics.i_brand_id, ics.i_class_id, ics.i_category_id
    FROM catalog_sales cs
    JOIN item ics ON cs.cs_item_sk = ics.i_item_sk
    JOIN date_dim d2 ON cs.cs_sold_date_sk = d2.d_date_sk
    WHERE d2.d_year BETWEEN 1998 AND 2000
      AND ics.i_category IN ('Books','Home','Jewelry')
      AND ics.i_manager_id BETWEEN 28 AND 37
      AND cs.cs_wholesale_cost BETWEEN 80 AND 100
),
web_brands AS (
    SELECT DISTINCT iws.i_brand_id, iws.i_class_id, iws.i_category_id
    FROM web_sales ws
    JOIN item iws ON ws.ws_item_sk = iws.i_item_sk
    JOIN date_dim d3 ON ws.ws_sold_date_sk = d3.d_date_sk
    WHERE d3.d_year BETWEEN 1998 AND 2000
      AND ws.ws_wholesale_cost BETWEEN 80 AND 100
),
cross_triples AS (
    SELECT s.i_brand_id, s.i_class_id, s.i_category_id
    FROM store_brands s
    JOIN catalog_brands c
      ON s.i_brand_id = c.i_brand_id
     AND s.i_class_id = c.i_class_id
     AND s.i_category_id = c.i_category_id
    JOIN web_brands w
      ON s.i_brand_id = w.i_brand_id
     AND s.i_class_id = w.i_class_id
     AND s.i_category_id = w.i_category_id
),
cross_items AS (
    SELECT i.i_item_sk
    FROM item i
    JOIN cross_triples ct
      ON i.i_brand_id = ct.i_brand_id
     AND i.i_class_id = ct.i_class_id
     AND i.i_category_id = ct.i_category_id
    WHERE i.i_category IN ('Books','Home','Jewelry')
      AND i.i_manager_id BETWEEN 28 AND 37
),
avg_sales AS (
    SELECT AVG(quantity * list_price) AS average_sales
    FROM (
        SELECT ss_quantity AS quantity, ss_list_price AS list_price
        FROM store_sales
        JOIN date_dim ON ss_sold_date_sk = d_date_sk
        WHERE d_year BETWEEN 1998 AND 2000
          AND ss_wholesale_cost BETWEEN 80 AND 100
        UNION ALL
        SELECT cs_quantity, cs_list_price
        FROM catalog_sales
        JOIN date_dim ON cs_sold_date_sk = d_date_sk
        WHERE d_year BETWEEN 1998 AND 2000
          AND cs_wholesale_cost BETWEEN 80 AND 100
        UNION ALL
        SELECT ws_quantity, ws_list_price
        FROM web_sales
        JOIN date_dim ON ws_sold_date_sk = d_date_sk
        WHERE d_year BETWEEN 1998 AND 2000
          AND ws_wholesale_cost BETWEEN 80 AND 100
    ) x
),
target_weeks AS (
    SELECT d_week_seq,
           CASE WHEN d_year = 1999 THEN 'this' ELSE 'last' END AS year_type
    FROM date_dim
    WHERE (d_year = 1999 AND d_moy = 12 AND d_dom = 5)
       OR (d_year = 1998 AND d_moy = 12 AND d_dom = 5)
),
combined_sales AS (
    SELECT i.i_brand_id,
           i.i_class_id,
           i.i_category_id,
           SUM(CASE WHEN tw.year_type = 'this' THEN ss.ss_quantity * ss.ss_list_price ELSE 0 END) AS ty_sales,
           SUM(CASE WHEN tw.year_type = 'this' THEN 1 ELSE 0 END) AS ty_number_sales,
           SUM(CASE WHEN tw.year_type = 'last' THEN ss.ss_quantity * ss.ss_list_price ELSE 0 END) AS ly_sales,
           SUM(CASE WHEN tw.year_type = 'last' THEN 1 ELSE 0 END) AS ly_number_sales
    FROM store_sales ss
    JOIN item i ON ss.ss_item_sk = i.i_item_sk
    JOIN date_dim d ON ss.ss_sold_date_sk = d.d_date_sk
    JOIN target_weeks tw ON d.d_week_seq = tw.d_week_seq
    JOIN cross_items ci ON ss.ss_item_sk = ci.i_item_sk
    WHERE i.i_category IN ('Books','Home','Jewelry')
      AND i.i_manager_id BETWEEN 28 AND 37
      AND ss.ss_wholesale_cost BETWEEN 80 AND 100
    GROUP BY i.i_brand_id, i.i_class_id, i.i_category_id
)
SELECT 'store' AS ty_channel,
       cs.i_brand_id AS ty_brand,
       cs.i_class_id AS ty_class,
       cs.i_category_id AS ty_category,
       cs.ty_sales AS ty_sales,
       cs.ty_number_sales AS ty_number_sales,
       'store' AS ly_channel,
       cs.i_brand_id AS ly_brand,
       cs.i_class_id AS ly_class,
       cs.i_category_id AS ly_category,
       cs.ly_sales AS ly_sales,
       cs.ly_number_sales AS ly_number_sales
FROM combined_sales cs
CROSS JOIN avg_sales av
WHERE cs.ty_sales > av.average_sales
  AND cs.ly_sales > av.average_sales
  AND cs.ty_number_sales > 0
  AND cs.ly_number_sales > 0
ORDER BY ty_channel, ty_brand, ty_class, ty_category
LIMIT 100;

      AND ss.ss_wholesale_cost BETWEEN 80 AND 100

catalog_brands AS (

    FROM catalog_sales cs

    JOIN date_dim d2 ON cs.cs_sold_date_sk = d2.d_date_sk

    JOIN date_dim d3 ON ws.ws_sold_date_sk = d3.d_date_sk
    WHERE d3.d_year BETWEEN 1998 AND 2000
      AND ws.ws_wholesale_cost BETWEEN 80 AND 100
),
cross_triples AS (
    SELECT s.i_brand_id, s.i_class_id, s.i_category_id
    FROM store_brands s
    JOIN catalog_brands c
      ON s.i_brand_id = c.i_brand_id
     AND s.i_class_id = c.i_class_id
     AND s.i_category_id = c.i_category_id
    JOIN web_brands w
      ON s.i_brand_id = w.i_brand_id
     AND s.i_class_id = w.i_class_id
     AND s.i_category_id = w.i_category_id
),
cross_items AS (
    SELECT i.i_item_sk AS ss_item_sk
    FROM item i
    JOIN cross_triples ct
      ON i.i_brand_id = ct.i_brand_id
     AND i.i_class_id = ct.i_class_id
     AND i.i_category_id = ct.i_category_id
    WHERE i.i_category IN ('Books','Home','Jewelry')
      AND i.i_manager_id BETWEEN 28 AND 37
),
avg_sales AS (
    SELECT AVG(quantity * list_price) AS average_sales
    FROM (
        SELECT ss_quantity AS quantity, ss_list_price AS list_price
        FROM store_sales
        JOIN date_dim ON ss_sold_date_sk = d_date_sk
        WHERE d_year BETWEEN 1998 AND 2000
          AND ss_wholesale_cost BETWEEN 80 AND 100

        FROM catalog_sales

        JOIN date_dim ON ws_sold_date_sk = d_date_sk
        WHERE d_year BETWEEN 1998 AND 2000
          AND ws_wholesale_cost BETWEEN 80 AND 100
    ) x
),
target_weeks AS (
    SELECT d_week_seq,
           CASE WHEN d_year = 1999 THEN 'this' ELSE 'last' END AS year_type
    FROM date_dim
    WHERE (d_year = 1999 AND d_moy = 12 AND d_dom = 5)
       OR (d_year = 1998 AND d_moy = 12 AND d_dom = 5)
),
combined_sales AS (
    SELECT i.i_brand_id,
           i.i_class_id,
           i.i_category_id,
           SUM(CASE WHEN tw.year_type = 'this' THEN ss.ss_quantity * ss.ss_list_price ELSE 0 END) AS ty_sales,
           SUM(CASE WHEN tw.year_type = 'this' THEN 1 ELSE 0 END) AS ty_number_sales,
           SUM(CASE WHEN tw.year_type = 'last' THEN ss.ss_quantity * ss.ss_list_price ELSE 0 END) AS ly_sales,
           SUM(CASE WHEN tw.year_type = 'last' THEN 1 ELSE 0 END) AS ly_number_sales
    FROM store_sales ss
    JOIN item i ON ss.ss_item_sk = i.i_item_sk
    JOIN date_dim d ON ss.ss_sold_date_sk = d.d_date_sk
    JOIN target_weeks tw ON d.d_week_seq = tw.d_week_seq
    JOIN cross_items ci ON ss.ss_item_sk = ci.ss_item_sk

      AND i.i_manager_id BETWEEN 28 AND 37

SELECT 'store' AS ty_channel,
       cs.i_brand_id AS ty_brand,
       cs.i_class_id AS ty_class,
       cs.i_category_id AS ty_category,
       cs.ty_sales AS ty_sales,
       cs.ty_number_sales AS ty_number_sales,
       'store' AS ly_channel,
       cs.i_brand_id AS ly_brand,
       cs.i_class_id AS ly_class,
       cs.i_category_id AS ly_category,
       cs.ly_sales AS ly_sales,
       cs.ly_number_sales AS ly_number_sales
FROM combined_sales cs
CROSS JOIN avg_sales av
WHERE cs.ty_sales > av.average_sales
  AND cs.ly_sales > av.average_sales
  AND cs.ty_number_sales > 0
  AND cs.ly_number_sales > 0
ORDER BY ty_channel, ty_brand, ty_class, ty_category
LIMIT 100;

    JOIN date_dim d3 ON ws.ws_sold_date_sk = d3.d_date_sk
    WHERE d3.d_year BETWEEN 1998 AND 2000
      AND ws.ws_wholesale_cost BETWEEN 80 AND 100
),
date_weeks AS (
    SELECT MAX(CASE WHEN d_year = 1999 AND d_moy = 12 AND d_dom = 5 THEN d_week_seq END) AS this_week_seq,
           MAX(CASE WHEN d_year = 1998 AND d_moy = 12 AND d_dom = 5 THEN d_week_seq END) AS last_week_seq
    FROM date_dim
),
agg AS (
    SELECT i.i_brand_id,
           i.i_class_id,
           i.i_category_id,
           SUM(ss.ss_quantity * ss.ss_list_price) FILTER (WHERE d.d_week_seq = w.this_week_seq) AS ty_sales,
           COUNT(*) FILTER (WHERE d.d_week_seq = w.this_week_seq) AS ty_number_sales,
           SUM(ss.ss_quantity * ss.ss_list_price) FILTER (WHERE d.d_week_seq = w.last_week_seq) AS ly_sales,
           COUNT(*) FILTER (WHERE d.d_week_seq = w.last_week_seq) AS ly_number_sales
    FROM store_sales ss
    JOIN item i ON ss.ss_item_sk = i.i_item_sk
    JOIN date_dim d ON ss.ss_sold_date_sk = d.d_date_sk
    JOIN cross_keys ck
      ON i.i_brand_id = ck.brand_id
     AND i.i_class_id = ck.class_id
     AND i.i_category_id = ck.category_id
    CROSS JOIN date_weeks w
    WHERE i.i_category IN ('Books', 'Home', 'Jewelry')
      AND i.i_manager_id BETWEEN 28 AND 37
      AND ss.ss_wholesale_cost BETWEEN 80 AND 100
      AND d.d_week_seq IN (w.this_week_seq, w.last_week_seq)
    GROUP BY i.i_brand_id, i.i_class_id, i.i_category_id
)
SELECT 'store' AS ty_channel,
       a.i_brand_id AS ty_brand,
       a.i_class_id AS ty_class,
       a.i_category_id AS ty_category,
       a.ty_sales,
       a.ty_number_sales,
       'store' AS ly_channel,
       a.i_brand_id AS ly_brand,
       a.i_class_id AS ly_class,
       a.i_category_id AS ly_category,
       a.ly_sales,
       a.ly_number_sales
FROM agg a
CROSS JOIN avg_sales av
WHERE a.ty_sales > av.average_sales
  AND a.ly_sales > av.average_sales
  AND a.ty_number_sales > 0
  AND a.ly_number_sales > 0
ORDER BY ty_channel, ty_brand, ty_class, ty_category
LIMIT 100; ##
## Rewrite Proposals: [{'group': 'Subquery_optimization', 'produced_suggestion': 'Removed the materialized cross_items item_sk CTE; the final query now joins item directly to a distinct cross_keys set (brand/class/category) derived from the three channel INTERSECTs. </analysis>
Comparing the three plans:

- **Original plan**: The outer query relies on two CTEs (`cross_items` and `avg_sales`), each consumed multiple times (the `cross_items` CTE is scanned twice via two parallel GroupAggregate subtrees, and `avg_sales` is scanned twice as InitPlans 4 and 6). The `cross_items` CTE itself is very expensive (~1,428,913), and the final stage duplicates the store_sales fact-table access (two Bitmap Heap Scans on `store_sales` plus two HashAggregates) to compute the two yearly sums independently.

- **Rewritten plan**: The query is restructured to a single Nested Loop whose inner side is one GroupAggregate using `FILTER` clauses to compute both year's sums in a single pass, with the average produced by one Finalize Aggregate consumed directly (no CTE materialization/re-scan). The `cross_items` computation is inlined as the right side of a Merge Join rather than a separately materialized CTE, and the final `Sort` for the LIMIT is eliminated.

- **Enhanced plan**: Structurally and cost-wise identical to the rewritten plan (every node matches exactly), meaning the enhancement pass could not find further optimization opportunities beyond the rewrite — the plan has converged.

Key quantitative comparisons:
- Total plan cost is essentially unchanged: 2,830,823.13 → 2,831,535.94 (+0.03%); startup cost drops slightly (2,830,823.12 → 2,845,545.21/2,828,545.21, about −0.08%). The LIMIT endpoint range widens (single-row estimate preserved), so the overall cost is cost-neutral at the planner level.
- The dominant node changes shape: the most expensive node shifts from the `cross_items` Hash Join (≈1,428,913, ~50% of total) to the inlined `SetOp Intersect` branch (≈1,421,058, a ~0.5% reduction on that subtree) plus a new top GroupAggregate (≈1,431,627).
- The real gains are structural: elimination of duplicate scans of `store_sales`, duplicate aggregate work, CTE re-materialization, and the final Sort.
</analysis>

</report>
1. Cost Efficiency
- Overall cost change: essentially flat, from 2,830,823.13 (original) to 2,831,535.94 (rewritten/enhanced), a nominal increase of ~0.03%, while the startup cost of the Limit node decreases by ~0.08% (2,830,823.12 → 2,828,545.21). The LIMIT's estimated cost range widens from a single point to a 2,991-cost span, reflecting a one-row Nested Loop estimate rather than a fixed-point Limit.
- Cost at the most expensive plan node: the original plan's dominant cost (~1,428,913, roughly half the total) sat in the `cross_items` Hash Join CTE subtree. In the rewritten/enhanced plan this intersect branch is reduced slightly to ~1,421,058 (−0.5%), and the most expensive node becomes the top-level GroupAggregate (~1,431,627). The optimizer-level costs are nearly identical, but the plan now performs less duplicated physical work for the same estimated cost (e.g., one pass over store_sales per group instead of two, and no CTE double-reads), which should favor real-world runtime even though the planner's estimate does not reward it.

2. Plan Characteristics
- Scan type transitions:
  - The final-stage access to `store_sales` changes from two Bitmap Heap/Index Scans (`idx_store_sales_item`) driven by the cross-items list to a single Index Scan on `idx_store_sales_sold_date` driven by the date dimension, converting the access pattern from item-key probing to date-key probing.
  - Two InitPlan Index Scans on `date_dim` (for 1999-12-05 and 1998-12-05) are replaced by one Aggregate over a Seq Scan on `date_dim` computing `max(CASE ...)` targets, with a Materialize node caching the single-row result.
  - A new Parallel Seq Scan on `date_dim` appears in the outer aggregation branch (previously date lookups there were pure index-driven).
- Join algorithm refinements:
  - The final join between the per-group sales aggregates and the intersected item set changes from a Nested Loop with a three-column Join Filter (over CTE scans) to a three-key Merge Join, with an explicit Sort supplying the merge order — a better match for the sorted intersect output.
  - The duplicate GroupAggregate/Nested Loop branches are collapsed into a single GroupAggregate whose join filter applies both year conditions via FILTER aggregates in one pass.
- Elimination of explicit sorting and reduction of intermediate result sets:
  - The final `Sort` feeding the LIMIT is removed entirely.
  - The `HashAggregate` over the `cross_items` CTE scan (and the CTE itself as a materialized boundary) is eliminated; the intersect result flows directly into the Merge Join.
  - The duplicated store_sales aggregation (two separate Bitmap scans, two Sorts, two GroupAggregates in the original outer stage) is merged into a single aggregation, roughly halving the intermediate result volume of the final stage.

3. Resource Utilization
- Memory usage: the original plan built six Hash tables for the `date_dim` year-range joins (three in `cross_items`, three in `avg_sales`); the rewritten plan retains these (the avg_sales computation is unchanged) but removes the additional HashAggregate over the CTE and the two Sort nodes in the duplicated outer aggregates, trading them for one small Materialize node and one Sort feeding the Merge Join. Net hash-memory demand is slightly lower, and duplicated sorting work is eliminated.
- Worker count: parallelism is preserved — 2 workers remain in the three Gather Merge branches of the intersect computation and the 2-worker Finalize Aggregate for the average; the new date_dim-driven branch uses 1 worker. Total planned workers are effectively unchanged, with one added small parallel branch.

4. Other Improvements
- CTE elimination: both `cross_items` and `avg_sales` are no longer materialized and re-scanned; the intersect result is joined inline, and the average is consumed directly by the join filter, removing repeated CTE scan overhead and pipeline stalls.
- Single-pass conditional aggregation: `sum(...) FILTER (WHERE ...)` computes both years' sums in one aggregation instead of two separate subplans, and `count(*) FILTER` guards correctness — reducing fact-table touch count in the final stage from two passes to one.
- The enhanced plan is byte-for-byte identical to the rewritten plan, indicating the rewrite already reached a plan-shape local optimum: no further scan, join, or parallelism refinements were available to the enhancer.
- Recommendation: since planner cost is cost-neutral but structural work (duplicate scans, sorts, CTE materialization) is reduced, validating with actual execution (EXPLAIN ANALYZE) is advised to confirm wall-clock and buffer-usage gains that the estimated costs do not reflect.
</report>

Let's produce the final answer.agg. But that would reintroduce multiple scans. Alternatively, we can define base CTEs as sales+date_dim only, and join item later. That avoids the inner join to item for avg_sales. Let's consider that.

Option B: base CTEs only sales+date_dim filtered by year and wholesale cost.
```
store_base AS (
  SELECT ss.ss_quantity AS quantity,
         ss.ss_list_price AS list_price,
         ss.ss_item_sk,
         ss.ss_wholesale_cost,
         d.d_week_seq
  FROM store_sales ss
  JOIN date_dim d ON ss.ss_sold_date_sk = d.d_date_sk
  WHERE d.d_year BETWEEN 1998 AND 2000
    AND ss.ss_wholesale_cost BETWEEN 80 AND 100
)
```
Similarly catalog_base, web_base.
Then avg_sales uses these directly (no item join).
cross_keys:
```
SELECT i.i_brand_id, i.i_class_id, i.i_category_id
FROM store_base sb
JOIN item i ON sb.ss_item_sk = i.i_item_sk
WHERE i.i_category IN (...) AND i.i_manager_id BETWEEN 28 AND 37
INTERSECT
SELECT i.i_brand_id, ...
FROM catalog_base cb
JOIN item i ON cb.cs_item_sk = i.i_item_sk
WHERE ...
INTERSECT
SELECT i.i_brand_id, ...
FROM web_base wb
JOIN item i ON wb.ws_item_sk = i.i_item_sk
```
final agg:
```
FROM store_base sb
JOIN item i ON sb.ss_item_sk = i.i_item_sk
JOIN cross_keys ck ON i.i_brand_id = ck.brand_id ...
WHERE i.i_category IN (...) AND i.i_manager_id BETWEEN ...
```
This scans base CTEs (materialized) multiple times, but item join happens per use. Since item is small (102k rows), joining it multiple times against materialized base CTE might be okay. However, if store_base is large (millions), joining item multiple times (once for cross_keys, once for agg) could be costly. But cross_keys is an INTERSECT which will process many rows and join to item. If store_base already had item attributes, we would avoid that join. The main cost is likely scanning the large base CTE. Joining to item is a hash join with small table, usually fast. But doing it twice might add overhead. Yet, including item in base CTE means the materialized CTE is wider and larger. The join to item is done once during base CTE creation. Which is better? If base CTE is used 3 times for store (avg_sales, cross_keys, agg), including item avoids joining item 2 times (cross_keys and agg) but adds item columns to materialization. Since item join is cheap, either is fine. The corrected suggestion says "materialize each filtered fact table once in a CTE and reuse it across the average computation, the INTERSECT key list, and the weekly aggregation". It implies the CTE should have the necessary columns for all uses, which includes item attributes. So including item is probably intended.

Let's think about PostgreSQL CTE materialization. If we define `store_base` with item join, it will execute the join once and materialize the result. The result includes item columns. The number of rows is the number of store_sales rows with year 1998-2000 and wholesale cost 80-100. How many rows is that? Based on data_statistics, store_sales total 28,800,991. Date range in TPC-DS is typically 1998-2003? Actually TPC-DS uses dates from 1998 to 2003 for sales? Let me recall: TPC-DS date_dim has dates from 1900-01-01 to 2100-01-01? The sales data is generated for years 1998-2003? I think TPC-DS sales dates are within 1998-2003 (6 years). So 3 years is half of sales: ~14.4M rows. Wholesale cost 80-100 might filter a small percentage. In the plan, store_sales after wholesale filter is 2,239,677. That's for all dates. Then date join year 1998-2000 filters to 31,564 according to planner, but that's likely a misestimate. If actual sales dates are only 1998-2003, then the year filter 1998-2000 might select about half of the wholesale-filtered rows, so ~1.1M rows. That's still large but manageable. Materializing 1.1M rows for store, plus maybe 0.6M for catalog, 0.3M for web, total ~2M rows. That's not huge. Materializing once and reusing should be faster than scanning 2.2M multiple times. So including item is fine.

But wait: The plan's wholesale filter estimate of 2.2M for store_sales is after filtering `ss_wholesale_cost between 80 and 100`. In TPC-DS, wholesale_cost is randomly distributed? It might be uniform between 1 and 100? If uniform, 80-100 is 20% of range. 28.8M * 0.2 = 5.76M. The plan estimate 2.2M might be based on something else. Anyway, it's a few million.

Now, could we avoid avg_sales entirely by noticing it's a constant? No, it must be computed.

Let's consider if we can compute avg_sales and cross_keys in a single pass without materializing base CTEs. We could use a UNION ALL of the three fact tables with a channel flag, and then compute both avg_sales and cross_keys using conditional aggregation and grouping sets? That's complex. The base CTE approach is standard.

Let's write the final SQL with base CTEs. We need to be careful with aliases and column names.

SQL:
```
WITH
store_base AS (
    SELECT ss.ss_quantity AS quantity,
           ss.ss_list_price AS list_price,
           ss.ss_item_sk,
           ss.ss_wholesale_cost,
           d.d_week_seq,
           i.i_brand_id,
           i.i_class_id,
           i.i_category_id,
           i.i_category,
           i.i_manager_id
    FROM store_sales ss
    JOIN item i ON ss.ss_item_sk = i.i_item_sk
    JOIN date_dim d ON ss.ss_sold_date_sk = d.d_date_sk
    WHERE d.d_year BETWEEN 1998 AND 2000
      AND ss.ss_wholesale_cost BETWEEN 80 AND 100
),
catalog_base AS (
    SELECT cs.cs_quantity AS quantity,
           cs.cs_list_price AS list_price,
           cs.cs_item_sk,
           cs.cs_wholesale_cost,
           d.d_week_seq,
           i.i_brand_id,
           i.i_class_id,
           i.i_category_id,
           i.i_category,
           i.i_manager_id
    FROM catalog_sales cs
    JOIN item i ON cs.cs_item_sk = i.i_item_sk
    JOIN date_dim d ON cs.cs_sold_date_sk = d.d_date_sk
    WHERE d.d_year BETWEEN 1998 AND 2000
      AND cs.cs_wholesale_cost BETWEEN 80 AND 100
),
web_base AS (
    SELECT ws

.ws_quantity AS quantity,
           ws.ws_list_price AS list_price,

    JOIN item i ON ws.ws_item_sk = i.i_item_sk

    WHERE d.d_year BETWEEN 1998 AND 2000
      AND ws.ws_wholesale_cost BETWEEN 80 AND 100

cross_keys AS (
    SELECT i_brand_id AS brand_id,
           i_class_id AS class_id,
           i_category_id AS category_id
    FROM store_base
    WHERE i_category IN ('Books', 'Home', 'Jewelry')
      AND i_manager_id BETWEEN 28 AND 37

avg_sales AS (
    SELECT AVG(quantity * list_price) AS average_sales
    FROM (
        SELECT quantity, list_price FROM store_base
        UNION ALL
        SELECT quantity, list_price FROM catalog_base
        UNION ALL
        SELECT quantity, list_price FROM web_base
    ) x
),
date_weeks AS (
    SELECT MAX(CASE WHEN d_year = 1999 AND d_moy = 12 AND d_dom = 5 THEN d_week_seq END) AS this_week_seq,
           MAX(CASE WHEN d_year = 1998 AND d_moy = 12 AND d_dom = 5 THEN d_week_seq END) AS last_week_seq
    FROM date_dim
),
agg AS (
    SELECT sb.i_brand_id,
           sb.i_class_id,
           sb.i_category_id,
           SUM(CASE WHEN sb.d_week_seq = w.this_week_seq THEN sb.quantity * sb.list_price END) AS ty_sales,
           COUNT(CASE WHEN sb.d_week_seq = w.this_week_seq THEN 1 END) AS ty_number_sales,
           SUM(CASE WHEN sb.d_week_seq = w.last_week_seq THEN sb.quantity * sb.list_price END) AS ly_sales,
           COUNT(CASE WHEN sb.d_week_seq = w.last_week_seq THEN 1 END) AS ly_number_sales
    FROM store_base sb
    JOIN cross_keys ck
      ON sb.i_brand_id = ck.brand_id
     AND sb.i_class_id = ck.class_id
     AND sb.i_category_id = ck.category_id
    CROSS JOIN date_weeks w
    WHERE sb.i_category IN ('Books', 'Home', 'Jewelry')
      AND sb.i_manager_id BETWEEN 28 AND 37
      AND sb.d_week_seq IN (w.this_week_seq, w.last_week_seq)
    GROUP BY sb.i_brand_id, sb.i_class_id, sb.i_category_id
)
SELECT 'store' AS ty_channel,
       a.i_brand_id AS ty_brand,
       a.i_class_id AS ty_class,
       a.i_category_id AS ty_category,
       a.ty_sales,
       a.ty_number_sales,
       'store' AS ly_channel,
       a.i_brand_id AS ly_brand,
       a.i_class_id AS ly_class,
       a.i_category_id AS ly_category,
       a.ly_sales,
       a.ly_number_sales
FROM agg a
CROSS JOIN avg_sales av
WHERE a.ty_sales > av.average_sales
  AND a.ly_sales > av.average_sales
ORDER BY ty_channel, ty_brand, ty_class, ty_category
LIMIT 100;
```

Potential issue: The original web branch in cross_items does not filter by i_category or i_manager_id, but it does join item and filter `ws_wholesale_cost` and `d3.d_year`. Our web_base filters those and joins item. It includes all items. So selecting brand/class/category from web_base is correct.

Potential issue: The original cross_items outer query selects `i_item_sk ss_item_sk from item, (intersect keys) x where i_brand_id = brand_id ... and i_category IN ... and i_manager_id BETWEEN ...`. But wait: Could there be an item with the same brand/class/category but different manager that is in cross_keys? cross_keys was computed from store/catalog with manager filter, so any key in cross_keys came from at least one store/catalog item with manager 28-37 and category in set. But there could be multiple items with the same brand/class/category but different manager. The final store_base row we are considering has its own manager. We filter sb.i_manager_id BETWEEN 28 AND 37. So only items with manager 28-37 are included. If an item with manager outside range has the same brand/class/category, it will be excluded by our filter. In the original, cross_items only includes item_sk of items that themselves satisfy manager filter. Then final uses ss_item_sk in cross_items and also joins item and filters manager. So an item with manager outside range would not be in cross_items. So both exclude. If an item with manager inside range has the same brand/class/category as another item with manager outside, both are in cross_keys? cross_keys is just the key, so it doesn't distinguish manager. But our final filters manager, so only inside range items are included. Original cross_items includes only inside range item_sk. So equivalent.

What about the fact that cross_keys is computed from all store/catalog items with manager filter, but final store_sales might be for an item with manager inside range whose brand/class/category is in cross_keys. That's exactly the condition. So equivalent.

Now, consider if we can avoid including `i_category` and `i_manager_id` in base CTEs for web, but it's fine.

Let's check if there is any issue with `COUNT(CASE WHEN ... THEN 1 END)`. This counts non-null values. For rows where condition is false, it returns NULL, which is not counted. So it counts rows for that week. Equivalent to `COUNT(*) FILTER (WHERE ...)`. Good.

Now, what about the `avg_sales` CTE? It uses `store_base`, `catalog_base`, `web_base`. These base CTEs filter by year and wholesale cost, and also join item. As discussed, inner join to item is safe. ```sql
WITH
store_base AS (
    SELECT ss.ss_quantity AS quantity,
           ss.ss_list_price AS list_price,
           d.d_week_seq,
           i.i_brand_id,
           i.i_class_id,
           i.i_category_id,
           i.i_category,
           i.i_manager_id
    FROM store_sales ss
    JOIN item i ON ss.ss_item_sk = i.i_item_sk
    JOIN date_dim d ON ss.ss_sold_date_sk = d.d_date_sk
    WHERE d.d_year BETWEEN 1998 AND 2000
      AND ss.ss_wholesale_cost BETWEEN 80 AND 100
),
catalog_base AS (
    SELECT cs.cs_quantity AS quantity,
           cs.cs_list_price AS list_price,
           i.i_brand_id,
           i.i_class_id,
           i.i_category_id,
           i.i_category,
           i.i_manager_id
    FROM catalog_sales cs
    JOIN item i ON cs.cs_item_sk = i.i_item_sk
    JOIN date_dim d ON cs.cs_sold_date_sk = d.d_date_sk
    WHERE d.d_year BETWEEN 1998 AND 2000
      AND cs.cs_wholesale_cost BETWEEN 80 AND 100
),
web_base AS (
    SELECT ws.ws_quantity AS quantity,
           ws.ws_list_price AS list_price,
           i.i_brand_id,
           i.i_class_id,
           i.i_category_id
    FROM web_sales ws
    JOIN item i ON ws.ws_item_sk = i.i_item_sk
    JOIN date_dim d ON ws.ws_sold_date_sk = d.d_date_sk
    WHERE d.d_year BETWEEN 1998 AND 2000
      AND ws.ws_wholesale_cost BETWEEN 80 AND 100
),
cross_keys AS (
    SELECT i_brand_id AS brand_id,
           i_class_id AS class_id,
           i_category_id AS category_id
    FROM store_base
    WHERE i_category IN ('Books', 'Home', 'Jewelry')
      AND i_manager_id BETWEEN 28 AND 37
    INTERSECT
    SELECT i_brand_id,
           i_class_id,
           i_category_id
    FROM catalog_base
    WHERE i_category IN ('Books', 'Home', 'Jewelry')
      AND i_manager_id BETWEEN 28 AND 37
    INTERSECT
    SELECT i_brand_id,
           i_class_id,
           i_category_id
    FROM web_base
),
avg_sales AS (
    SELECT AVG(quantity * list_price) AS average_sales
    FROM (
        SELECT quantity, list_price FROM store_base
        UNION ALL
        SELECT quantity, list_price FROM catalog_base
        UNION ALL
        SELECT quantity, list_price FROM web_base
    ) x
),
date_weeks AS (
    SELECT MAX(CASE WHEN d_year = 1999 AND d_moy = 12 AND d_dom = 5 THEN d_week_seq END) AS this_week_seq,
           MAX(CASE WHEN d_year = 1998 AND d_moy = 12 AND d_dom = 5 THEN d_week_seq END) AS last_week_seq
    FROM date_dim
),
agg AS (
    SELECT sb.i_brand_id,
           sb.i_class_id,
           sb.i_category_id,
           SUM(CASE WHEN sb.d_week_seq = w.this_week_seq THEN sb.quantity * sb.list_price END) AS ty_sales,
           COUNT(CASE WHEN sb.d_week_seq = w.this_week_seq THEN 1 END) AS ty_number_sales,
           SUM(CASE WHEN sb.d_week_seq = w.last_week_seq THEN sb.quantity * sb.list_price END) AS ly_sales,
           COUNT(CASE WHEN sb.d_week_seq = w.last_week_seq THEN 1 END) AS ly_number_sales
    FROM store_base sb
    JOIN cross_keys ck
      ON sb.i_brand_id = ck.brand_id
     AND sb.i_class_id = ck.class_id
     AND sb.i_category_id = ck.category_id
    CROSS JOIN date_weeks w
    WHERE sb.i_category IN ('Books', 'Home', 'Jewelry')
      AND sb.i_manager_id BETWEEN 28 AND 37
      AND sb.d_week_seq IN (w.this_week_seq, w.last_week_seq)
    GROUP BY sb.i_brand_id, sb.i_class_id, sb.i_category_id
)
SELECT 'store' AS ty_channel,
       a.i_brand_id AS ty_brand,
       a.i_class_id AS ty_class,
       a.i_category_id AS ty_category,
       a.ty_sales,
       a.ty_number_sales,
       'store' AS ly_channel,
       a.i_brand_id AS ly_brand,
       a.i_class_id AS ly_class,
       a.i_category_id AS ly_category,
       a.ly_sales,
       a.ly_number_sales
FROM agg a
CROSS JOIN avg_sales av
WHERE a.ty_sales > av.average_sales
  AND a.ly_sales > av.average_sales
ORDER BY ty_channel, ty_brand, ty_class, ty_category
LIMIT 100;

[
    {
        "group": "Query_optimization",
        "produced_suggestion": "Consolidate repeated scans of store_sales, catalog_sales and web_sales: each large fact table is scanned twice (once for cross_items, once for avg_sales) plus an extra scan of store_sales for the final aggregation. Materialize each filtered fact table once in a base CTE (year 1998-2000, wholesale_cost 80-100, joined to item/date_dim) and reuse it across cross_keys, avg_sales and the final aggregation."
    },
    {
        "group": "Subquery_optimization",
        "produced_suggestion": "Replace the materialized item_sk list (cross_items) with an intersection of distinct (brand, class, category) keys (cross_keys), then join the final aggregation directly on those keys. This removes an extra item lookup and the IN subquery while preserving semantics since the outer item filters (category, manager) are applied in the final aggregation."
    },
    {
        "group": "Query_optimization",
        "produced_suggestion": "Merge the two nearly identical this_year / last_year store_sales aggregations into a single scan with conditional aggregation (SUM/COUNT CASE on d_week_seq), replacing the costly self-join between two aggregate subqueries."
    },
    {
        "group": "Join_optimization",
        "produced_suggestion": "Convert all comma-style joins into explicit INNER JOIN syntax to avoid accidental Cartesian products and improve readability; keep the web branch of cross_keys free of category/manager filters to match the original semantics."
    },
    {
        "group": "Constant_folding",
        "produced_suggestion": "Fold the expressions 1998 + 2 -> 2000 and 1998 + 1 -> 1999 at rewrite time."
    },
    {
        "group": "Predicate_simplification",
        "produced_suggestion": "Collapse the two scalar d_week_seq subqueries into a single date_weeks CTE computing both week sequences in one pass over date_dim; the redundant i_category/i_manager_id filters that appeared both in cross_items and the outer query are retained only where they are semantically needed (cross_keys store/catalog branches and final aggregation)."
    }
]
</advice>

</analysis>
The main bottleneck is that store_sales, catalog_sales and web_sales are each scanned two or three times with identical predicates (d_year 1998-2000, wholesale_cost 80-100): once for cross_items, once for avg_sales, and store_sales again for both the this-year and last-year aggregations. The previous rewrite was cost-neutral because it never addressed these repeated scans.

Strategy scores:
1. Base CTE consolidation of each fact table (9/10) — scan each sales table once, materialize filtered rows, and reuse for cross_keys, avg_sales and the final weekly aggregation. This cuts actual I/O roughly in half despite planner cost estimates that understate the date join selectivity.
2. Merging this/last year aggregations (7/10) — one pass over store_base with conditional SUM/COUNT replaces two aggregate scans and an inner join; null filtering on ty_sales/ly_sales > average_sales exactly reproduces the inner join semantics.
3. cross_keys replacing cross_items (6/10) — intersecting distinct brand/class/category triples avoids materializing item_sk lists and an extra item join; the outer item filters are pushed into the store/catalog branches of cross_keys and the final aggregation.
4. Explicit JOIN syntax (5/10) — safety/readability.
5. Constant folding (3/10) and date_weeks CTE (2/10) — minor.

Equivalence notes: the web branch of cross_keys intentionally omits category/manager filters (as in the original); the item inner join added to the base CTEs is one-to-one on the sales foreign keys, so avg_sales is unchanged; filtering a.ty_sales/ly_sales > average_sales in the final WHERE reproduces the HAVING clauses and the inner join between this_year and last_year.
</analysis>

</enhanced_sql>
```sql

           i_category_id
    FROM catalog_base
    WHERE i_category IN ('Books', 'Home', 'Jewelry')
      AND i_manager_id BETWEEN 28 AND 37
    INTERSECT
    SELECT i_brand_id,
           i_class_id,
           i_category_id
    FROM web_base
),
avg_sales AS (
    SELECT AVG(quantity * list_price) AS average_sales
    FROM (
        SELECT quantity, list_price FROM store_base
        UNION ALL
        SELECT quantity, list_price FROM catalog_base
        UNION ALL
        SELECT quantity, list_price FROM web_base
    ) x
),
date_weeks AS (
    SELECT MAX(CASE WHEN d_year = 1999 AND d_moy = 12 AND d_dom = 5 THEN d_week_seq END) AS this_week_seq,
           MAX(CASE WHEN d_year = 1998 AND d_moy = 12 AND d_dom = 5 THEN d_week_seq END) AS last_week_seq
    FROM date_dim
),
agg AS (
    SELECT sb.i_brand_id,
           sb.i_class_id,
           sb.i_category_id,
           SUM(CASE WHEN sb.d_week_seq = w.this_week_seq THEN sb.quantity * sb.list_price END) AS ty_sales,
           COUNT(CASE WHEN sb.d_week_seq = w.this_week_seq THEN 1 END) AS ty_number_sales,
           SUM(CASE WHEN sb.d_week_seq = w.last_week_seq THEN sb.quantity * sb.list_price END) AS ly_sales,
           COUNT(CASE WHEN sb.d_week_seq = w.last_week_seq THEN 1 END) AS ly_number_sales
    FROM store_base sb
    JOIN cross_keys ck
      ON sb.i_brand_id = ck.brand_id
     AND sb.i_class_id = ck.class_id
     AND sb.i_category_id = ck.category_id
    CROSS JOIN date_weeks w
    WHERE sb.i_category IN ('Books', 'Home', 'Jewelry')
      AND sb.i_manager_id BETWEEN 28 AND 37
      AND sb.d_week_seq IN (w.this_week_seq, w.last_week_seq)
    GROUP BY sb.i_brand_id, sb.i_class_id, sb.i_category_id
)
SELECT 'store' AS ty_channel,
       a.i_brand_id AS ty_brand,
       a.i_class_id AS ty_class,
       a.i_category_id AS ty_category,
       a.ty_sales,
       a.ty_number_sales,
       'store' AS ly_channel,
       a.i_brand_id AS ly_brand,
       a.i_class_id AS ly_class,
       a.i_category_id AS ly_category,
       a.ly_sales,
       a.ly_number_sales
FROM agg a
CROSS JOIN avg_sales av
WHERE a.ty_sales > av.average_sales
  AND a.ly_sales > av.average_sales
ORDER BY ty_channel, ty_brand, ty_class, ty_category
LIMIT 100;
```

</analysis>
Both rewrites correctly restructure the query into shared CTEs, single-pass conditional aggregation for the two "weeks", and a single join back against avg_sales, avoiding repeated scans of avg_sales. ID 0 is preferable because:
- It materializes item attributes (brand/class/category) once in `store_base`, so the final aggregation does not need to re-join `item`, reducing one large join compared to ID 1's `agg` CTE.
- It uses portable `SUM(CASE WHEN ...)`/`COUNT(CASE WHEN ...)` conditional aggregation rather than Postgres-specific `FILTER (WHERE ...)` clauses, making it valid across engines (MySQL, Spark SQL, etc.).
- Its `cross_keys` faithfully preserves the original INTERSECT semantics (no category/manager filter on the web branch), same as ID 1, but with a cleaner, pre-filtered base.
- It consistently applies both `ty_sales > avg AND ly_sales > avg` thresholds, matching the original HAVING logic applied to both derived tables.

ID 1 is logically equivalent but carries an extra item join and dialect-specific FILTER syntax, giving no additional benefit.

## Selected Rewritten SQL: WITH
store_base AS (
    SELECT ss.ss_quantity AS quantity,
           ss.ss_list_price AS list_price,
           d.d_week_seq,
           i.i_brand_id,
           i.i_class_id,
           i.i_category_id,
           i.i_category,
           i.i_manager_id
    FROM store_sales ss
    JOIN item i ON ss.ss_item_sk = i.i_item_sk
    JOIN date_dim d ON ss.ss_sold_date_sk = d.d_date_sk
    WHERE d.d_year BETWEEN 1998 AND 2000
      AND ss.ss_wholesale_cost BETWEEN 80 AND 100
),
catalog_base AS (
    SELECT cs.cs_quantity AS quantity,
           cs.cs_list_price AS list_price,
           i.i_brand_id,
           i.i_class_id,
           i.i_category_id,
           i.i_category,
           i.i_manager_id
    FROM catalog_sales cs
    JOIN item i ON cs.cs_item_sk = i.i_item_sk
    JOIN date_dim d ON cs.cs_sold_date_sk = d.d_date_sk
    WHERE d.d_year BETWEEN 1998 AND 2000
      AND cs.cs_wholesale_cost BETWEEN 80 AND 100
),
web_base AS (
    SELECT ws.ws_quantity AS quantity,
           ws.ws_list_price AS list_price,
           i.i_brand_id,
           i.i_class_id,
           i.i_category_id
    FROM web_sales ws
    JOIN item i ON ws.ws_item_sk = i.i_item_sk
    JOIN date_dim d ON ws.ws_sold_date_sk = d.d_date_sk
    WHERE d.d_year BETWEEN 1998 AND 2000
      AND ws.ws_wholesale_cost BETWEEN 80 AND 100
),
cross_keys AS (
    SELECT i_brand_id AS brand_id,
           i_class_id AS class_id,
           i_category_id AS category_id
    FROM store_base
    WHERE i_category IN ('Books', 'Home', 'Jewelry')

    FROM web_base
),

    FROM (
        SELECT quantity, list_price FROM store_base
        UNION ALL
        SELECT quantity, list_price FROM catalog_base
        UNION ALL
        SELECT quantity, list_price FROM web_base
    ) x
),
date_weeks AS (
    SELECT MAX(CASE WHEN d_year = 1999 AND d_moy = 12 AND d_dom = 5 THEN d_week_seq END) AS this_week_seq,
           MAX(CASE WHEN d_year = 1998 AND d_moy = 12 AND d_dom = 5 THEN d_week_seq END) AS last_week_seq
    FROM date_dim
),
agg AS (
    SELECT sb.i_brand_id,
           sb.i_class_id,
           sb.i_category_id,
           SUM(CASE WHEN sb.d_week_seq = w.this_week_seq THEN sb.quantity * sb.list_price END) AS ty_sales,
           COUNT(CASE WHEN sb.d_week_seq = w.this_week_seq THEN 1 END) AS ty_number_sales,
           SUM(CASE WHEN sb.d_week_seq = w.last_week_seq THEN sb.quantity * sb.list_price END) AS ly_sales,
           COUNT(CASE WHEN sb.d_week_seq = w.last_week_seq THEN 1 END) AS ly_number_sales
    FROM store_base sb
    JOIN cross_keys ck
      ON sb.i_brand_id = ck.brand_id
     AND sb.i_class_id = ck.class_id
     AND sb.i_category_id = ck.category_id
    CROSS JOIN date_weeks w
    WHERE sb.i_category IN ('Books', 'Home', 'Jewelry')
      AND sb.i_manager_id BETWEEN 28 AND 37
      AND sb.d_week_seq IN (w.this_week_seq, w.last_week_seq)
    GROUP BY sb.i_brand_id, sb.i_class_id, sb.i_category_id
)
SELECT 'store' AS ty_channel,
       a.i_brand_id AS ty_brand,
       a.i_class_id AS ty_class,

<analysis>
Comparing the three EXPLAIN plans:

1. **Original plan (ori)**: Total top-level cost = 2,830,823.13. It contains two expensive CTEs: `cross_items` (cost up to 1,428,913.26) and `avg_sales` (cost up to 1,399,908.73). Both CTEs independently re-scan the three large fact tables (store_sales, catalog_sales, web_sales) via Parallel Seq Scans. The `avg_sales` CTE duplicates the exact same Parallel Seq Scan + Hash Join pipelines that already exist inside `cross_items`, meaning the fact tables are effectively scanned twice. The cross-channel intersection uses nested `SetOp Intersect` fed by `Gather Merge` + `Sort` nodes. Many parallel branches (3 Gather Merge nodes + 1 Gather node, each with 2 workers) and multiple Sort nodes are required.

2. **Rewritten plan (re)**: Total top-level cost = 1,447,124.40 — a ~48.9% reduction. It restructures the query into three reusable base CTEs (`store_base`, `catalog_base`, `web_base`) that materialize each channel's filtered sales joined to item and date_dim **once**. The intersection is then computed via `HashSetOp Intersect` over cheap CTE Scans instead of re-deriving from raw fact tables, and the average-sales aggregation is computed by an `Aggregate` over a simple `Append` of CTE scans (cost only 4,503.92 instead of 1,399,908.73 — a ~99.7% reduction at that node). A `Memoize` node is introduced to cache repeated item lookups by `ss_item_sk`.

3. **Enhanced plan (imp)**: It is structurally and numerically **identical** to the rewritten plan (same nodes, same costs 1,447,124.32..1447124.40). The enhancement attempt produced no further optimizer changes — the rewritten plan is already the converged, optimal shape for this query under the current indexes and statistics.

Key observations for the report:
- Overall cost change: 2,830,823.13 → 1,447,124.40 (−48.9%).
- Most expensive node: original `avg_sales` Partial Aggregate branch (1,398,908.50) / `cross_items` sort branches (up to 708,081.40 per-worker sorts; 693,102.20 parallel scans) → rewritten most expensive node is the single Parallel Seq Scan on store_sales (693,102.20), and the duplicated aggregation pipeline is reduced to 4,503.92.
- Scan transitions: no Seq→Index flip on the big fact tables (they remain parallel seq scans, correctly so given the low-selectivity wholesale_cost filter), but the *number* of times they are scanned is halved. item lookups use index scans with Memoize caching; date_dim consistently uses idx_date_dim_year_moy.
- Sort elimination: all `Sort` nodes feeding the original Gather Merge → SetOp pipeline are removed; HashSetOp needs no explicit per-branch sorting in the plan.
- Workers: both plans use 2 workers, but the original instantiates parallelism in 4 separate subtrees (some duplicated work), while the rewritten plan parallelizes only the three base CTE builds; downstream operations are serial but cheap.
</analysis>

<report>
## Query Optimization Comparison Report

### 1. Cost Efficiency

- **Overall cost change**: The top-level plan cost dropped from **2,830,823.13** in the original plan to **1,447,124.40** in the rewritten plan, a reduction of approximately **48.9%** (nearly half the original estimated cost).
- **Cost change at the most expensive plan node**:
  - In the original plan, the dominant cost came from the duplicated fact-table pipelines: the `avg_sales` CTE alone cost **1,399,908.73** (built on a Parallel Append of three Hash Joins over full parallel scans of store_sales, catalog_sales, and web_sales), and the `cross_items` CTE cost another **1,428,913.26**, repeating essentially the same scans and joins.
  - In the rewritten plan, the equivalent average-sales computation is served by a simple `Aggregate → Append → CTE Scan` costing only **4,503.92** — a reduction of roughly **99.7%** at that node — because it reuses already-materialized base CTEs instead of re-scanning the fact tables. The most expensive remaining node is a single Parallel Seq Scan on store_sales (693,102.20), which is unavoidable given the low-selectivity wholesale-cost filter.
- **Enhanced plan**: The enhanced EXPLAIN is byte-for-byte identical to the rewritten plan (same node tree, same cost bounds), indicating the rewrite has already reached the optimizer's fixed point; no further cost improvement was attainable at the plan level.

### 2. Plan Characteristics

- **Scan type transitions**:
  - The large fact tables (store_sales, catalog_sales, web_sales) remain Parallel Seq Scans in both plans — appropriate, since the wholesale-cost range filter retains ~2.2M rows out of each table, so an index would not pay off.
  - The decisive improvement is **eliminating duplicate scans**: the original plan scanned each fact table twice (once for `cross_items`, once for `avg_sales`); the rewritten plan scans each exactly once inside `store_base` / `catalog_base` / `web_base`.
  - Item-dimension access uses `idx_item_sk` Index Scans in both, but the rewritten plan wraps the store-side lookup in a **Memoize** node (logical cache keyed on `ss_item_sk`), avoiding repeated index probes for repeated item keys.
  - date_dim access consistently leverages `idx_date_dim_year_moy` in all versions.
- **Join algorithm refinements**:
  - The cross-channel intersection changed from nested **SetOp Intersect** fed by three `Gather Merge → Sort` pipelines (which forced sorted inputs from parallel workers) to **HashSetOp Intersect** fed directly by cheap CTE Scans — removing the requirement to sort each channel's output before the set operation.
  - The final comparison logic was restructured from two independent GroupAggregate subtrees joined via a Nested Loop with an equality Join Filter into a single GroupAggregate joined through a Hash Join on (brand, class, category), with the manager/category predicates pushed down as filters on the CTE scans rather than applied during raw fact-table joins.
- **Elimination of sorting and intermediate result reduction**:
  - All three per-worker `Sort` nodes inside the original `cross_items` Gather Merge branches are gone; the corresponding ~470K and ~708K sort costs disappear.
  - The `HashAggregate` over `cross_items` CTE scans (in the original final block) is no longer needed since the intersection is materialized once in the CTE.
  - The separate `avg_sales` pipeline (Parallel Append + Partial/Finalize Aggregate with 2 workers) is entirely eliminated, collapsing a full second pass over ~138K aggregated rows into a trivial re-read of materialized CTEs.

### 3. Resource Utilization

- **Memory usage**:
  - The original plan builds multiple Hash tables for the same date_dim lookup (three inside `cross_items` plus three inside `avg_sales` — six total hash builds of the same ~1,078-row date set), whereas the rewritten plan builds it only three times (once per base CTE).
  - Explicit Sort nodes in the original (with their spilling/tape overhead on large intermediate sets) are fully removed; the only remaining sort in the rewritten plan is a tiny single-row sort (cost 9,817.14 → one row) feeding the GroupAggregate.
  - The Memoize node introduces a small, bounded cache that trades a bit of memory for avoiding repeated item index probes — a favorable trade given the wide fan-out from store_sales.
- **Worker count adjustment**:
  - Both plans plan **2 workers**, but the original instantiated parallelism in four distinct subtrees (three Gather Merge branches for the intersection plus one Gather for the average aggregation), causing parallel startup/teardown overhead and duplicated CPU work. The rewritten plan concentrates parallelism in the three one-time base CTE builds; everything downstream is serial but operates on tiny materialized intermediates, so effective parallel efficiency is higher despite the same nominal worker count.

### 4. Other Improvements

- **CTE materialization reuse**: The central win — the three base CTEs (`store_base`, `catalog_base`, `web_base`) are computed once and consumed multiple times (intersection, average computation, and the final comparison), eliminating the "compute twice" anti-pattern of the original plan.
- **Predicate placement**: The `i_manager_id BETWEEN 28 AND 37` and category-list filters were moved from the fact-table join stage into filters on the narrow CTE scans, reducing the width of data flowing into the set operations.
- **Convergence**: Since the enhanced plan is identical to the rewritten plan, the rewrite represents the stable optimum under the current schema/indexes; any further gains would require physical changes (e.g., partial indexes on wholesale_cost ranges or materialized views over the channel aggregates) rather than plan-level rewriting.
</report>

    "reason": "The enhanced SQL is a valid semantic rewrite: it replaces the two separate this_year/last_year scans of store_sales with a single conditional aggregation over shared CTEs, factors the INTERSECT cross-item logic into a reusable cross_keys CTE, and precomputes the week sequences via date_weeks. It preserves the original filters (year range, wholesale cost, category, manager_id, category IN list) and the HAVING conditions (both ty_sales and ly_sales > average_sales), and produces the same output columns and ordering. Consolidating repeated scans and subqueries should reduce I/O and join work substantially, so the rewrite is judged an improvement and the optimization can be terminated."
